## L3 staged-rise prototype rejection and original rise records

At exact source revision `e9d7e644b9f8c1cbade9434919f58be8a71ad000`
on 2 October, a disposable native runtime spread rises above four pixels
over successive ticks at two vertical pixels per tick while horizontal
movement continued. Rises of up to four pixels retained the tracked rule.
The substituted source, compiled library, fixture-audit executable and
audit log SHA-256 values were
`36f19666d28f7ec2ffeaabad8fcc54a316037002172f539ce4436c46bf3f534a`,
`a3521b83632ded30faaf7d4b2e7a1028e6d310e898e3100de459bd37003e21af`,
`9f1924f841ad6a1fd2f566f3c662554937c2194b9c7a414337cbead0e1ecdbbc`
and `1ee02e23993e23a755d2eaa449ee7b2acd2ce97095b40e8123702c4fe90cfd56`.
Only 16 of 41 retained L3 fixtures passed; 25 failed. This prototype is
rejected. Do not repeat it on unchanged source and data.

An offline read of the supplied `GRAPHICS/LEMDATA3.DAT` (SHA-256
`7b7f6cd447539797bc9b40aa3e61bdcf0c1060bb39d83ab477739380ce8550cf`)
found four rise records for each direction and phase. The second-overlay
handler at code offset `0x5dc7` selects them when the actor's `+0x14`
field is 1, 2, 3 or 4. One right-facing set is at data offsets `0x174`,
`0x19e`, `0x1c8` and `0x1f2`; its record headers specify anchor deltas
`(+8,-2)`, `(+8,-4)`, `(+8,-6)` and `(+8,-8)`. The other right-facing
set starts at `0x224`; left-facing sets start at `0x2d4` and `0x384`
with matching negative horizontal deltas. The eight-pixel rise records
contain five frame entries; the others contain four. These are original
movement-data and dispatcher facts, not proof that the ordinary walker
uses every rise record. The setter for actor field `+0x14`, the terrain
probe and the exact contact tick still need a matched original-engine
trace before a tracked runtime rule can be changed. No engine, route,
manifest or gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and
both stay Preview.

## L3 original walker-to-rise control path

At exact source revision `87caf6073893eed4b92838e30d167184adf93d6a`
on 2 October, a further offline read of the same original `L3CD.EXE`
(SHA-256 `5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`)
traced a rise decision from terrain handling to the movement records.
The second-overlay routine at code offset `0xc91a` samples terrain near
the actor's animation anchor. At `0xcb97` it derives the actor's `+0x14`
field from one terrain result and selects action `8`. The action table at
`0x72b6` dispatches action `8` to `0x50c7`; that handler selects state
`9` when the current state is `0x1c`. The state table at `0x73ea` sends
state `9` to the rise-record selector at `0x5dc7`. Action `0` also selects
state `0x1c` for actor mode `0`, and that state's handler uses the phased
horizontal movement records. This establishes a connected original
walking-to-rise path; the earlier uncertainty about whether those records
can be reached from walking is resolved.

The terrain helper at `0xc7a6` counts up to four samples into `+0x14`,
but other terrain helpers also set that field. The exact sample geometry,
terrain bit meanings, actor anchor versus visible foot position, and tick
ordering still need a matched original-engine trace. The native runtime's
one-pixel walker cannot safely adopt the original eight-pixel animation
record delta without those details: three tested approximations invalidated
16, 29 or all 41 retained fixtures, and the staged-rise trial invalidated
25. No tracked engine, route, manifest or gate changed. L2 remains
75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original anchor and visible-frame timing

At exact source revision `73d35a584cfc08b15c861af9082f398ebfb6ab7b`
on 2 October, the original animation records and the second-overlay
frame queue were read together. The record loader at code offset `0x7abf`
adds its horizontal and vertical deltas to pending movement fields `+4`
and `+6`. The actor loop at `0x3cd4` applies those pending deltas to the
anchor fields `+8` and `+0xa` only when the queued frame count `+0xca`
reaches zero. The display update at `0x7266` then reads frame entries in
reverse order and adds their offsets to that anchor for the visible
position at fields `+0` and `+2`.

For the right-facing flat records at data offsets `0x0000` and `0x002a`,
the first record queues a later `+8` anchor delta and visible x offsets
`-2,-1,0,+1` in playback order. The second queues no anchor delta and
visible offsets `-6,-5,-4,-3`. Across the record boundary these produce
continuous one-pixel visible steps after the anchor advances by eight.
The rise records at `0x174` through `0x1f2` use phased x offsets
and add vertical frame offsets before their pending `-2` through `-8`
anchor deltas are applied. The eight-pixel rise takes five queued frames;
smaller rises take four. These values come from the supplied original
movement data, not a native gameplay trace.

The native one-pixel visible walking speed is therefore consistent with
these original records. The unresolved difference is the original
contact probe's anchor-relative position and frame phase, including
terrain-bit interpretation. Changing native visible speed or staging a
rise without that model has already broken retained routes. No engine,
route, fixture, manifest or gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains
41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original rise-sampler bit classes

At exact tracked source revision
`34207aa0199a0cfe0d9e4b404aaf4d1e40c56f03` on 2 October,
an offline disassembly of the supplied DOS CD executable examined the
second overlay's rise path and terrain sampler. The executable SHA-256
was `5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`.
The temporary disassembly source and log SHA-256 values were
`3c19d30096564d6592a53b81fb057c752671e614a3321c0b325f7c92f7016453`
and `8f6e259e9c28240d2f10057f8c445a164511ab8b06afc814e1e29021f9207bb9`.

The terrain sampler at overlay offset `0x7597` takes four samples two
vertical pixels apart. It returns `0x20` for no selected bits, `0x40`
for a partial mask and `0x80` for all four samples. Its mode table
maps mode 4 to bit mask `0x10000000` and mode 0 to mask `0x00200088`.
In the rise path at `0xc9dd`, the engine samples eight anchor pixels
ahead and 14 pixels above the anchor with mode 4. It then samples the
same position with mode 0. Each branch requires result `0x20` before
the path continues. This supports a class-specific projected probe,
but the mapping from the native 16-bit scene tags to the original
32-bit cells and the actor-anchor alignment remain unverified.

A headless native contact probe found four `0x0060` projected tags at
the Classic `020` eight-pixel wall and four `0x1000` tags at the Classic
`016` six-pixel wall. The temporary probe source, executable and
two-contact log SHA-256 values were
`4860c3b262d1a487812385bf285b001a48e83c82dfb0451ef36d79b7e30e80f7`,
`828ff20896fc1f42a2d52c442bef7dc761982d08612788c647199b2b09e69e0e`
and `11597325bc9f216352e4cd599181fc3d5f6ee0faf05af6c34be23354361d86ba`.
The native `isSolid` check uses tag bit `0x20`, which does not model
both original sampler modes. No new rule passed original-engine parity
or the strict replay gate in this batch. No tracked engine, route,
fixture, manifest or gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains
41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original terrain-cell layout check

At exact tracked source revision
`75b477196f4770d9d1153e05bf300b227bdb772c` on 2 October,
an offline read of the relocated DOS CD main image and the supplied
second overlay traced the terrain lookup used by the rise sampler.
The main image SHA-256 was
`e83edd76861cadbed109960f0c7e1e88eb91e33ce666962a74ad33904e301a12`.
The temporary disassembly source and log SHA-256 values were
`f8ddd9a60d0c3b6f0c3601f3e70ac8db71e9e6c37db37544b7d752192dd0112b`
and `997d117d25ee47acc85bb792532bc065251021a63fa5e514cfb545e6d8eb4c38`.

The main lookup at offset `0x5b77` divides y by two and x by eight,
then multiplies the x column by four. It returns a pointer to a
32-bit terrain cell. In the second overlay's cell assembly at `0x264f`,
the engine reads a separate 32-bit source value, copies its `dh` byte
into `ah` and tests source bits. The branch at `0x26e0` sets or clears
cell bit `0x10000000` according to `ah & 4`, after tests of `ah & 1`
and existing cell bits `0x70`.

This establishes that the original mode-4 cell bit is assembled from
source fields and cell state. A direct shift from the native 16-bit
`0x1000` tag is not established. The rejected phase-0 class-probe
prototype used that unproved mapping. The source fields, native tag
mapping and actor anchor need further comparison before a safe rule
change. No tracked engine, route, fixture, manifest or gate changed.
L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original terrain-operation queue and rise bit

At exact tracked source revision
`311a95868d967a1bd71c6adef24a2164b22f7763` on 3 October,
an offline 16-bit disassembly of the supplied DOS CD executable
traced the source of the terrain-cell bit tested by the original rise
sampler. `L3CD.EXE` SHA-256 remains
`5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`.
The second overlay's routine at code offset `0x2311` appends 12-byte
operation records. Its first dword comes from `edx`; the next two come
from `eax` and `ecx`. The terrain-cell assembly at `0x2625` processes
these records in reverse order. It copies bits 8–15 of the first dword
into `ah`, calls the terrain lookup, then sets or clears cell bit
`0x10000000`
according to `ah & 4` when `ah & 1` is clear and cell bits `0x70`
are clear.

The two local callers at `0x2730` and `0x2780` construct that high
byte from their arguments. The first starts with `dh = 2` and adds
mask `0x04` when its argument at `[bp+8]` is nonzero. The second starts
with `dh = 1` and adds mask `0x04` when its `[bp+8]` is nonzero, alongside
other operation flags. This establishes an operation-record path to
the original mode-4 cell bit. It does not establish which caller built
each cell in the two compared levels or a mapping from the supplied
16-bit object tags to the final 32-bit terrain grid. The lookup call's
effect on `ah` also remains untraced. In particular,
the rejected prototype's direct interpretation of native tag
`0x1000` remains unsupported.

The temporary 16-bit assembly and disassembly SHA-256 pairs were
`0229e7c16c87094152086904e1f62f3bcd6e2cc741c018d9d56226d568907603`
and `df54fe185fa5cf1a8e4d7cc14442b55f2822f8e90952a1340ca2baa43c76b21d`
for the cell loop, `998165ca7ec3a9766e724eedf7fbacf96a8acf0f421e2fdcef769e91fc6ae10b`
and `6dcf68a286a0b57d990281269336fa0f10f49ab5866e2333cefddc683e42f910`
for the record queue,
`d003d3a9beac967c82b0e5f83339675338a2f2be6df9705d763c3286729fcf37`
and `dd01704fd45057971b6c39f6e20d860360b7374c00f0543737635ea92b712ad1`
for the first caller, and
`0a22aa81b419ba727b72b63065065e672d3246747731e1791786dc158dcdb697`
and `cbd53ec1fab921eaac4dd841456674703fb7ddc7074ae315635b665d0b4eb361`
for the second caller. These files remain in `/tmp` and outside Git.
No native engine, route, fixture, manifest or exit gate changed. The
next comparison must connect original operation arguments to the
terrain cells at the Classic `020` and `016` contacts before changing
the rise rule. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 lookup-target check and Classic 026 / Egyptian 211 searches

At exact tracked source revision
`cb99f5a9b6395af96cc165f638540211724b0717` on 3 October,
a direct 16-bit disassembly of the supplied DOS CD executable at
second-overlay offset `0x5d95` exposed animation selection, not the

and `525b4bfcfdb9b21c9da52172bd0b37c9a3e84f99f8f4230be58afd4a627aa6b0`.

reached beam 256, expanded 865,685 states and found no win. Egyptian
`211` reached beam 1,024, expanded 640,824 states and found no win.
Both best partials saved 0 and lost 0. The level SHA-256 values were
`17ab2b1c30ba7b11d7b307c398b3e443956a5aa65b45dd9abfebdc589080da22`
and `8ac64f06259b503328eae6ae573e7e459ba7a025cce77f657041591a2927e363`.

`01b5bc2030183420d266ff93f3fc045d4bad734a0792cbadccdf9f2e6b2894fc`
and `16e9410b3dc68e5db64206eaa7b299291831fb29fcd0f751944979ebfda94586`.
The temporary candidate directory contains no route. These bounds do
not establish impossibility and should not be repeated on unchanged

## L3 phase-zero prototype replacement-route and parity audit

At exact tracked source revision
`e4622b0003667b347e6fe9d0498381cb5647cea8` on 3 October, the
second disposable phase-zero `0x0020` prototype above was searched
against Classic `003` and missing Shadow `115`. The source-built solver
SHA-256 was `7359795164277b39bdffeb9896ae17b281865c6fbecce6c3525d83e16e701f90`;
the full search log SHA-256 was
`1e81eca7bae7b97b0b91bbf601532d5e80cbcc7513864157217ced3917c5646a`.
With 45 seconds per level, initial beam 128 and fewer inputs first,
Classic `003` found a four-input route with 10 saved, 0 lost and 10 in
reserve at tick 465. The solver replayed the candidate twice against
the exact level. Its fixture SHA-256 was
`902e64475fa09413b9748d864dec074622157f3212497537289370ff0a230aa6`;
the embedded level SHA-256 was
`a8c0df1b5a2754a8ec452652dae5ceb63d75e53f1b2dc57af3b3af2a5d7f12fa`.
This improves the old Classic `003` result by one surviving lemming.
Shadow `115` found no win after 1,110,233 expanded states at beam 256;
its best partial saved 0 and lost 0. This bound does not establish
impossibility and must not be repeated on unchanged source and data.

An independent verification used a temporary copy of the 41 retained
fixtures with only Classic `003` replaced by that candidate. The
273-entry campaign manifest passed. Classic `003` then passed two more
exact-level replays. The complete gate stopped after Classic `010`
because the next retained route failed; its log SHA-256 was
`91fff111d08e9dadade35776a9a8f986e99ce785d7b10675671df218d6e253a6`.
An individual audit of every retained fixture found 32 passes and nine
failures: Classic `013` and `015`, Shadow `108` and `114`, and Egyptian
`209`, `210`, `215`, `222` and `223`. Its SHA-256 was
`3b3ff4fa9a651187bde70018044706f0c3440ae4ffb5f26c2e2bc62d6fe6bcef`.
The candidate is valid only for the disposable prototype; it is not an
accepted tracked route because the engine rule regresses those nine
wins. No engine, fixture or manifest was changed. L2 remains 75/120,
L3 remains 41/90, all broad exit gates remain open, and both games
stay Preview. The affected contacts should guide a narrower original
engine comparison before another production rise-rule change.

## L3 phase-zero contact classification audit

At exact tracked source revision
`53263b32cc619bb4aafd12dbe0ade4d59172ede4` on 3 October, a
disposable diagnostic build logged every rise above four pixels caused
by the rejected phase-zero `0x0020` prototype. It replayed the Classic
`003` candidate and all nine retained fixtures that prototype breaks.
Classic `003` passed; its four-input candidate did not take a logged
phase-zero rise. All nine retained fixtures failed and all nine took at
least one such rise. The first observed contacts were Classic `013`
at `(224,40)` and `015` at `(472,184)`, Shadow `108` at `(320,144)`
and `114` at `(168,74)`, and Egyptian `209` at `(224,384)`, `210` at
`(152,138)`, `215` at `(320,188)`, `222` at `(304,64)` and `223` at
`(520,196)`. Their four projected native tags included both all-`0x0020`
and mixed `0x1000`/`0x0020` patterns. Egyptian `222` took 217 logged
rises, so the bypass can change a retained route extensively.

The instrumented source, library and contact-log SHA-256 values were
`2f47ce7faf7632b35e6963df97ae2b807f6eafedb20d3d261223d1cd2df18750`,
`9b377a54a45aea9193e694a7b67d239fd66b87f89951c8609168e9e6e2291f02`
and `73d242d74991aee89b09858ef9916247061a3fd0acfa50b02c67b6f43af12b04`.
The earlier passive Classic `003` trace and some broken routes encounter
all-`0x0020` projected tags, so that native tag pattern alone cannot
distinguish the desired original rise. The original 32-bit cell assembly
and actor anchor still need a matched contact comparison. No production
rule, fixture, manifest or gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains
41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 phase-zero prototype replacement-route sweep

At exact tracked source revision
`455f45be05921492b6475461ef56e0e640f96f34` on 3 October, the
disposable phase-zero `0x0020` engine prototype searched all nine

expanded states, improving the retained 1-saved route by one survivor.
The remaining-eight pass found Shadow `114` with 1 saved, 10 lost and
10 in reserve after 1,155,517 states, also one survivor better. It
found Egyptian `210` with 6 saved and 0 in reserve after 625,044
states; this keeps only six survivors against ten in its retained
fixture, so it is unsuitable as a replacement.

No winning candidate appeared for Classic `015` (716,934 expanded),
Shadow `108` (539,667), or Egyptian `209` (1,036,434), `215`
(761,706), `222` (991,677) and `223` (1,260,964). These bounds do
not prove the routes impossible. The solver and prototype library
SHA-256 values were

and `e9006871d00bd3aff706e5b41aae6bc10a5f9a777a55e59671736c1aae4942d6`.

and `a82bfb3196cbe31335ae681558d25ca9fc9465c5ac7f6abd31984f0ef75b2d65`.

`7594a58d38775b264f5c04dcd843a507d56d9d64fb20ab3e4beb13b492e8f3e2`

The solver replayed each candidate twice against the exact supplied

the temporary candidates to the tracked manifest. The verification
log SHA-256 was
`a3d5d0b566518dcdeaf5974332483fa63c8432dbfe8628baa987fa5cf1e4e6f4`.
Classic `013` and Shadow `114` are verified prototype-only replacement

The prototype still fails the retained-route gate and remains outside

## L3 phase-zero retained-route repair audit

At exact tracked source revision
`00c373ea0dcec55696ed4999364ebc2c1a430530` on 3 October, a
disposable guided replay diagnostic tested the first new phase-zero
`0x0020` rise in each remaining broken fixture. The prototype library
SHA-256 remained
`e9006871d00bd3aff706e5b41aae6bc10a5f9a777a55e59671736c1aae4942d6`.
The final diagnostic source and executable SHA-256 values were
`b04f255be2b7af03c95a2c5567ce97bcf2fc1296c8a8dda76a81e01fedeef54c`
and `3795c57a76db04a38d1d2cf593bbdb160816bef586b3db20bf2dc505e05b5f52`.

Five retained routes still reached their original winning outcomes
under the prototype when replay inputs rejected by the changed state
were removed. The exact-level candidates preserved both saved and
reserve counts:

| Level | Accepted inputs / retained inputs | Saved / lost / reserve | Candidate SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Shadow `108` | 17 / 26 | 1 / 19 / 0 | `c5a334b07be28e3370ffdcf72b510dd3d6de40da474fb347cbc1bc8fd59a7ed7` |
| Egyptian `209` | 243 / 280 | 1 / 13 / 10 | `e31e3ac02a948442028b10d4bb662dc08f5ced2af71131b44bc5f9e4015d48ce` |
| Egyptian `210` | 14,263 / 42,464 | 1 / 11 / 9 | `6ab0f7199c8bbaa209503b617cab82c537ab92a7f7743f5152eaebcd13898e06` |
| Egyptian `215` | 69 / 72 | 2 / 19 / 0 | `3568a5eb692199d78db197a76a2dd29de8c0e519d2c1f8c3bcc0f83f4b2c5170` |
| Egyptian `223` | 229 / 247 | 1 / 11 / 10 | `fccb650ce371dcb8a98d4b6db84f3888cae1b5cc97d6fa81ba8d75159a8c2687` |

The diagnostic replayed each filtered candidate twice with exact
level data and initial-state identity. The separate completion verifier
then replayed each twice from temporary fixtures. The tracked 273-entry

With the earlier Classic `003`, `013` and Shadow `114` temporary
replacements, 39 of the 41 retained L3 routes passed the independent
prototype verifier. Classic `015` and Egyptian `222` remain broken.

For those two levels, one added walker, jumper or blocker input at each
tick within ten ticks of the first new rise found no win. A bounded
single-input removal or time shift by 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 or 32 ticks,
applied to every retained input, also found no win. Their retained
routes remain useful regression evidence. These probes do not prove
that either level lacks a winning route. The five filtered candidates
are verified **prototype-only** routes and are not tracked fixtures.
They do not increase the distinct-level proof-of-wins count or prove
original-engine parity. No production engine, fixture, manifest or
exit gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay
Preview.

## L3 Classic 015 prototype route and complete retained-route audit

At exact tracked source revision
`f2275029307995cc24aebd63eac925fe873c566c` on 3 October, an
offline, source-derived rise variant required the underlying temporary
object's uniform flags to equal `0x0020`. The contact in Classic `015`
comes from an object with uniform flags zero and an explicit `0x0020`
cell; this variant tested whether those sources should behave
differently. Its library SHA-256 was
`197a87aa66f3831e5e0ce40b62de8e10191c5139d59a5565ba879f58534fcf95`.
Only 36 of 41 temporary retained fixtures passed. Classic `015`,
Shadow `114`, and Egyptian `209`, `210` and `215` failed. The uniform
object flag does not isolate a valid original rule. This variant remains
disposable and must not be promoted or searched again on unchanged data.

A separate guided repair used the prior phase-zero `0x0020` prototype.
The tracked Classic `015` winner is lemming 1, saved at native tick 757.
Under the prototype, the first raised contact sends lemming 0 right;
this changes the terrain and displaces the eventual winner. Adding a
walker assignment for lemming 0 at tick 140 returned it towards the
lower route. Adding a walker assignment for lemming 1 at tick 600
restored the winner's direction before its existing tick-603 jump.
Moving its final jump from tick 722 to tick 723 produced the retained
outcome: 1 saved, 22 lost and none in reserve. All 37 inputs were
accepted. The exact level SHA-256 remained
`12fa2fee4174b01fd2de08aa19126cca5a85a0fbc0ebda216ba7dc5f99262e79`;
candidate-file SHA-256 was
`7a6b4f4a0ffc00cfacfa5afe43b3d168548c05874d6d97cf0065fc0427613cf5`.

The diagnostic replayed the candidate twice against the exact level
and initial-state identity. The independent completion verifier
replayed its temporary fixture twice. It then passed all 41 temporary
retained L3 fixtures in one batch; that log's SHA-256 was
`dcb9c743d1565a633f87c45c8487da8a8c0b6b99fad827b3978bc5be74c4efcb`.
The tracked 273-entry campaign manifest passed. The diagnostic source
and executable SHA-256 values were
`680b5792539667a7e19791f642d1f07f325528d9f1e21bb3cbd8d491aeed81a4`
and `e3918d18ffc18ef84bd2101b4d9d0f3e67265d43009358e9b427c9d85c32fe81`.
The `--require-all` verifier still reports 49 L3 levels without a
fixture, as expected; it is not a 90-level campaign pass.

This closes the **prototype's 41-route regression comparison**, not
the tracked engine's exit gate. The rise rule still needs original DOS
parity beyond the observed Classic `003` two-step path, followed by
production implementation and complete replay, campaign, media,
packaging and hardware evidence. No production engine, fixture,
manifest or exit gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90,
and both stay Preview.

`d3f310454a693255863bdec0683abd2c61d1feae` on 3 October, a

fixture to tick 2,000 with no input. It used the tracked engine and the
disposable phase-zero `0x0020` prototype on the same supplied level
data. Five levels differed in lemming 0's checkpoint path or the
saved/lost/reserve result: Classic `008`, Shadow `117`, and Egyptian
`211`, `221` and `227`. At tick 2,000, Classic `008` had 0 lost and 10
in reserve under the tracked engine, against 20 lost and none in
reserve under the prototype. Egyptian `221` likewise changed from
0 lost and 10 in reserve to 20 lost and none in reserve. Egyptian
`227` changed from 0 lost and 10 in reserve to 18 lost and none in
reserve. These passive outcomes are not winning-route evidence, but
show that the new rule can change unverified campaign levels sharply.
The comparison source, tracked log and prototype log SHA-256 values
were `3393e7919ac19ca04232d6badacb445ee93f6fceb63f5eeacf122527400b43a7`,
`4dc7dc14365c1f71afef16c8ec593d92e49cc63e64a5321f26fd98b1eec801af`
and `129b7bafe126b2a3a7aa4faa1ca84f2dddfcb7b46aeb13fbca210d439ec9abbf`.

The two changed actor paths with prior tracked no-win bounds were then
searched once under the changed prototype. Each used a 45-second
budget, beam 128 initially and fewer inputs first, with muted audio.
Shadow `117` expanded 932,385 states through beam 256; Egyptian `211`
expanded 968,566 states through beam 1,024. Neither found a win;
both best partials saved 0 and lost 0. The prototype solver and
library SHA-256 values were
`7359795164277b39bdffeb9896ae17b281865c6fbecce6c3525d83e16e701f90`
and `e9006871d00bd3aff706e5b41aae6bc10a5f9a777a55e59671736c1aae4942d6`.

and `f4888f65212049f6c9a8bd97acef9cecd2b0af193d7fbaf7012962714f259bc0`.
The temporary candidate directories are empty. These bounds do not

## L3 Shadow 15 left-stair and extra-direction diagnosis

supplied Shadow `115` level and a headless contact probe showed that
the native upper extra, when facing left, turns at x=232, y=56. The
next cell x=231 has an eight-pixel rise and native tag `0x0020`, with

x=231 has phase seven. The level file SHA-256 remains

## L3 Classic 29 rejected-action diagnosis and image-evidence correction

At exact tracked revision `34cf0369d178a8b50dd51de134600f9e4a367e4b`
on 4 October, a disposable headless probe replayed Classic `029`'s
tracked inputs under the direction-only prototype, skipping rejected
actions as in the route-repair trial. Immediately before input 14 at
tick 239, lemming 1 was at `(315, 338)`, walking left with no tool and
zero quantity. Its recorded `use upLeft` could not be accepted because
it had no bricks. The prototype starts both extra lemmings left:
`(24, 338)` and `(456, 336)`. The level has bricks at `(456, 306)`.
The changed direction plausibly prevents a pickup used by the old
route, but the probe does not establish the original game's initial
direction or a correct engine rule. The replay ended with 0 saved,
14 lost and 8 in reserve; 97 of its 389 inputs were rejected.

The earlier Shadow `115` image comparison overstated what an untimed
capture can prove. The first retained frame shows positions after an
unknown amount of simulation and possible turns. Its positions cannot
establish initial facing. The earlier sections now state this limit.
The direction-only prototype's 40 exact-replayed wins remain useful
diagnostic data, but they do not authorise a production direction
change. A timed original-engine trace is still needed before changing
that shared rule. A level-specific direction exception would have no
source basis.

## L3 extra-object source-definition check

At exact tracked revision `7428c51a96e5ee578e1f7f1ea5082ccfeb7e7d0c`
on 4 October, a headless probe decoded the supplied permanent style
banks for all three tribes. Extra-object IDs `10006` and `10007` have
one 16-by-16 frame each in every bank. Their definition flags are
`0x04ff` and `0x04fe` respectively; the one-bit difference and distinct
frame pixels confirm that the supplied data distinguishes the object
types. They do not identify which ID starts left or right. The native
loader's current even-left/odd-right mapping is consistent with its
creature-ID convention, but this convention is not a DOS rule trace.
The untimed Shadow `115` capture also cannot settle that mapping.

The probe source, executable and log SHA-256 values were
`54526dcd9b26c7027c96a94077080c470bbfc7087fc2054765726f4a5ce2ee79`,

headless and muted. No engine, fixture, manifest or exit gate changed.

## L3 extra-object pixel discriminator

At exact tracked revision `d85e801f1578e4c824b1328fd57d750643a65587`
on 4 October, the same decoded source frames were compared pixel by
pixel. IDs `10006` and `10007` differ at only 12 of 256 pixels, in
rows 3, 4, 6 and 7. In `10006`, the changed border-colour pixels lie
on the right at x=11–12; in `10007`, they lie on the left at x=3–4.
This is a directional visual cue in the supplied object art and gives
the reversed-extra prototype a stronger source-data lead than the
untimed DOSBox image. It remains an inference: the art does not prove
how the DOS code maps the object ID to an initial movement sign. The
prototype still lacks a Classic `029` win and original-engine timing.
No production direction rule is changed.

## Classic 28 original viewport alignment

## Timed original Shadow 15 extra path and direction trial

At exact tracked revision `b0a6fcfe30f884ecceed10588df8ef6de0d7eeef`
on 4 October, a disposable original-game copy loaded supplied Shadow
`115` as its opening level. Source and copy SHA-256 values matched for
`LEVEL115.DAT` (`973fb4edd55977ce4fa330f0c0bbbeb801e36dc4c27510c6211d699590ce5058`),
`PERM115.OBS` (`9369a67124d437efc9608f1cbddd69d360a5ce910e1a317a0cb96d687387342e`)
and `TEMP115.OBS` (`b9c425e550418cba1e52c797aab2cf12d8238f7d2d70f9a7cd48b5bed0304e71`).
DOSBox Staging ran with dummy video and audio, software output and
`nosound=true`; the injector only opened the level. Its 320-by-200
gameplay AVI decoded 626 frames. The AVI SHA-256 is
`55fa65e40a9e99a6644366fa164ea049ddd6745c5f2b8c6aa057466a1f5f1708`.

The source-built native scene aligns best with the original at viewport
y 0 across sampled gameplay frames, at 53.3%-53.9% exact RGB agreement.
The lower extra starts at native `(256,84)`. A blue-sprite centroid
trace of original frames moves from about `(259,84)` at frame 7 to
`(259,88)` at frame 60, then `(269,77)` at frame 91. This shows rightward
movement and ascent over the stepped terrain without a player tool.
The source marker `10006` points right in the Shadow object art, while
`10007` points left. These images support a direction-mapping problem,
although sprite-centroid positions are not exact logic positions or
tick alignment. The original upper extra's initial direction remains
unresolved because it can turn before the later sampled movement.

The tracked native loader starts `10006` left. A disposable build
started only `10006` right. On Shadow `115`, its lower extra reached
`(263,96)` facing right by tick 15, then turned at x 264 rather than
rising eight pixels. That exact contact has immediate tag `0x0020`,
four projected `0x0020` samples and solid terrain from y 88 through
y 95. Thus changing direction alone does not reproduce the original
path; the rise rule also needs a grounded correction. The disposable
runtime-source and library SHA-256 values were
`b982303b9c818569e083478323124bfcfd633f0e06ec68812b30b03951fa8d9f`
and `671aa360839f76dff2a612798a928d9da3c9b157c36d77d33384357de60dbf53`.

The strict L3 gate passed the tracked 273-entry campaign manifest but
rejected a changed fixture identity, as expected for a changed initial
direction. A diagnostic outcome-only pass applied each retained input
sequence, skipped rejected actions, and twice exactly replayed any
temporary win. It retained wins on 39 of 41 levels; Classic `013`
saved 0 and lost 13, and Classic `015` saved 0 and lost 23. This
diagnostic script is direct Swift execution because the repository
gate correctly rejects altered identities and has no outcome-only
trial mode. Its outcome log SHA-256 is
`17e83d0643d001fe2cbff4ac94a83df8cae1ef450ba1e10cd5f3e4b59bda35cc`.
The ignored `.build/sequel-reference/l3-shadow115-timed-20261004/`
directory holds the AVI, decoded frames, centroid script, native
scene, source object images, trial source and logs under a checked
`SHA256SUMS` manifest.

The original path is new evidence for diagnosing extra direction and
walking rise. The trial loses retained wins, so neither rule is
promoted. No candidate, production engine, fixture or exit gate
changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 extra direction correction and retained-route repair

Neither rise rule is promoted: the broad rule regresses retained

on unchanged model and level data. The ignored
`.build/sequel-reference/l3-phase0-broad-followup-20261004/` directory

The solver binary SHA-256 is
`f28f27596e5e490154fbe6910bf711a1e6e55c1a805db268c87f0de95140903d`.

library; repository scripts build the checked-in source.

Classic `015` still loses its retained win under the broad rule, and

tracked fixture, winning-route count or exit gate changed. L2 remains
75/120, L3 remains 41/90 and both remain Preview.

## L3 original Shadow 15 alignment and mixed plain-rise trial

At exact tracked revision `ba20470463f7fd2dc1c82b2153f49745ff240fd1`,
the existing headless original Shadow `115` capture was compared with
no-input native traces. The original lower extra's blue-sprite centroid
was sampled at frames 52–118. Fitting frame scale, frame offset and
sprite offsets on frames 52–85, then holding them fixed for frames
88–118, gave the broad and narrow plain-rise prototypes identical
held-out root-mean-square errors of 0.73 pixels in x and 1.62 in y.
The checked-in direction-only runtime gave 12.36 in x and 10.77 in y
under the same procedure. This supports an unassisted rise at the
lower extra's first `0x0020` step. It does not distinguish the broad
from the narrow rule or establish an exact original logic tick.

In the prior original Classic `003` no-input capture, a walker appears
near `(164,54)` in gameplay frame 540. On the broad prototype the
first extra reaches logic `(151,72)` and turns at x 152. That contact
has immediate native tag `0x0020` and projected tags
`[0x1000, 0x1000, 0x1000, 0x0020]`. A disposable variant allowing
phase-zero plain rises regardless of projected tags moved the same
actor through `(164,64)` at tick 144; the sprite's vertical offset is
consistent with the original image. This is spatial support for a
second rise, but the capture does not identify the actor or exact
tick well enough to prove the general terrain predicate.

Two disposable runtime rules were audited against all 41 retained L3
input sequences: any phase-zero `0x0020` contact, and a right-facing
rule admitting either four projected plain tags or the observed
three-`0x1000`, one-`0x0020` pattern. Each left 37 levels with a
twice-replayed exact win after rejected inputs were removed, but each
lost the retained wins on Classic `003`, Classic `015`, Shadow `114`
and Egyptian `222`. The mixed rule's extra contacts on Classic `015`
at `(240,268)` and `(256,378)` and Shadow `114` at `(168,74)` and
`(216,172)` share the observed projected-tag pattern. The trial
does not identify an original rule that separates these contacts.
Neither rule is promoted. The temporary runtime source SHA-256 values
are `1d8cad8afea775753389629a15eb74702fc3b539f0c8c329f26ccbae980e7b6e`
and `dab2b17122f0d0a61a234d00e6b741336c9097bfb9960fb44ad73354684b5a14`;
their library values are
`01d77690d246a620383458ac18cde3e02bf26023aa58f07958ebc60b35234453`
and `5deeafb5c8f459651e984ed20f7f39d8bd32b18c20909833805ad11c9f2dfa0f`.
The ignored `.build/sequel-reference/l3-plain-mixed-rise-20261004/`
directory contains both full outcome logs, paired traces, alignment
script and its checked `SHA256SUMS` file, SHA-256
`8205a02988652f523a8aa8d10be390fca34af7e1047541f4de401c178e362406`.
The tracked 273-entry campaign manifest still passed. Direct Swift
compilation was necessary to link the disposable runtime rules;
repository scripts build the checked-in engine.

No production engine, tracked fixture, winning-route count or exit
gate changed. The original plain-rise predicate, four retained-route
regressions, other missing routes and full campaign and package gates
remain open. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90; both remain Preview.

### 4 October disputed attribute-grid shape check

At exact tracked revision `b53d631f98306e076b7649bd0cf4e5ecdaa7940f`,

directly. Classic `015` object `118`, Shadow `114` object `421` and
Shadow `115` object `315` all use attribute-grid kind `1`, with one
16-bit tag per cell. Object `118` is a two-by-eight wedge whose upper
four rows have only the right `0x0020` cell. Object `315` is a
two-by-four wedge whose lower two rows have only the left `0x0020`
cell. Object `421` is a five-by-twelve stepped grid. Classic `003`
object `48` and Shadow `114` object `486` instead use uniform object
flags with no grid. The ignored evidence pack above now includes the
attribute-grid diagnostic log, whose SHA-256 is
`15620463f306b1c21b0e7aa93ecae87455fea4b1fcb34c2f47add28109023d63`.

Shadow `115` rise from the Classic `015` retained-route regression.

No production change, new route or gate resulted. L2 remains 75/120,
L3 remains 41/90 and both remain Preview.

### 4 October original Brick inventory correction and retained L3 replay gate

Starting from exact tracked revision `f993436bd6ba4560613730a5396bbafed133f0be`, the original DOS CD Classic tutorial's Brick pickup shows eight uses in the captured HUD. The source level SHA-256 is `2e62000858df2628606bbc99713bfe3e61fb2cf2afb80d221921e2bd9ff7b6cb`; the muted, headless DOSBox capture is `/private/tmp/l3-brick-hover-mid-20261004/captures/video0004.avi` (SHA-256 `2a0b2d209577547e202e2dd9af8650930ded6e0bc276c47c89f596688a54c2b2`), with frame 880 SHA-256 `30aa41e5697dcbfd8a06bdb54b93f631fe97e82870209dc648a3e900d77934e7`. The native L3 Brick pickup now supplies eight uses. Spade remains six and Grenade four. The runtime quantity assertions were updated accordingly.

The disposable eight-use trial initially retained only 8 of the 41 recorded L3 winning inputs unchanged. Bounded, level-specific repairs produced a candidate for every retained route; 33 tracked fixtures changed (25 for result identity alone and eight for input or outcome changes). Each candidate passed two exact-level replays with its source hash and saved/reserve result checked before promotion. The complete tracked `Scripts/run-lemmings3-runtime-tests.sh` suite passed headlessly with `LEMMINGS_TEST_AUDIO=muted`. `Tools/CampaignCompletion/report.py` regenerated a 273-entry manifest, and the tracked `Scripts/verify-l3-completion.sh` gate passed the manifest, all 41 distinct exact-level replays, and its missing fixture, population, hash, level number, outcome, rejected input and ordering negative checks. The gate still reports 49 L3 levels without a verified fixture. L2 remains 75/120 and L3 41/90; both remain Preview.

This establishes retention of the native winning fixtures under the corrected Brick quantity. Egyptian 02 and Shadow 08 currently need two Walker assignments to the same actor in one simulation tick. That is accepted by the native replay interface, but feasibility through the original DOS CD player input remains unconfirmed. Original-engine route parity, continuous campaign and ending progression, recovery and result identity, media and story, packaged app and hardware evidence remain open. Do not treat this local replay gate as the 1.8 exit gate.

### 5 October same-tick Walker route input audit

At exact source revision `6e35a35a3cdea6939dc47b23f5c1d8564766d71a`, the native input path was inspected for the two same-tick Walker assignments in the retained Shadow 08 and Egyptian 02 fixtures. `Lemmings3PlayWindow.applyAction` applies an accepted assignment immediately and records its current `game.tick`; it has no one-assignment-per-tick guard. The keyboard and pointer paths can call it more than once before `advanceTick`, and the simulation clock is 23 ticks per second. `Lemmings3Runtime.assign` accepts Walker on a builder to stop building, then accepts Walker again on the resulting walker to turn it. This establishes that the two fixture actions match a possible native input sequence. It does not prove that a human can reliably issue both within one tick or that the original DOS CD accepts the same sequence. No code, fixture or gate changed. Original input parity and the remaining 1.8 exit gates stay open; L2 remains 75/120 and L3 41/90, both Preview.

### 5 October paired-Walker solver branch and bounded check

It remains a solver branch; the game engine and fixture semantics are unchanged. It covered 905 passive decision points at depth 945, reached beam 256 across two rounds, expanded 1,164,063 states and found no win; the best partial saved 0 and lost 0. The candidate directory `/private/tmp/l3-paired-walker-classic20-20261005` is empty. Do not repeat this bound on unchanged source and level data. No new winning fixture or 1.8 exit gate closed. L2 remains 75/120 and L3 41/90, both Preview.

### 5 October Classic 15 control-source check

At exact tracked revision `9966db4bc2fdc90702e2278a2b3b1b855b9fd907`,

It describes scrolling when the pointer reaches the extreme left or
right edge and describes Walker turning a walking lemming. It does
not describe a vertical scroll control. This text reports Amiga use;
it is not a DOS CD control specification. The original DOS manual scan
at `https://db.hfsplay.fr/files/2018/10/06/c9f24f28-42dd-4046-aa9b-1d0b7622142e.pdf`
could be indexed but its control pages could not be fetched for

engine rule or route was promoted from this source check. The second
Classic 15 Walker and disputed rise remain unmatched; L2 remains
75/120 and L3 41/90, both Preview.

### 5 October original ending threshold

The supplied DOS `L3CD.EXE` (SHA-256
`5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`)
was unpacked from Microsoft EXEPACK for a bounded control-flow check. The
unpacked image SHA-256 is
`3bef59cedb516be505edaa42084fc9b136575f2964b7396cc5e3dc6de0544c04`.
The result writer at overlay offsets `0x38dc4`–`0x38e55` maps levels 1–30,
101–130 and 201–230 into three 31-byte groups. Their final-level result
slots are `0x1e`, `0x3d` and `0x5c`. At `0x2e957`, the game requires the
current tribe's final lemming count to be at least `0x32` (50) before marking
that tribe complete. At `0x2ed22`, it checks the other two final-level slots
against 50 before calling `THE-END.FLI`. The nearby original result strings
also state that 50 lemmings are required to complete a tribe.

The native flow previously treated 90 recorded level wins as full title
completion, irrespective of the three final survivor counts, and did not
automatically play `THE-END.FLI`. The original result threshold is now an
explicit 1.8 gate. The bounded binary check did not prove bit-for-bit DOS
carry arithmetic, so native survivor-count equivalence still needs campaign
traces.

### 5 October native finale flow

Native player progress now marks a tribe complete when its recorded final
result has at least 50 survivors. This follows the observed DOS ending
check even when an earlier level was skipped. The strict 1.8 replay gate
separately requires all 30 levels in each continuous tribe chain.
`THE-END.FLI` opens only after the result that first completes the third
eligible tribe. Finishing or skipping it returns to
the library or standalone game menu; missing or unreadable automatic media
also completes the handover. The pending ending is consumed before playback,
so a repeated Continue action cannot queue it again. An under-50 finale shows
the target and offers Choose level.

The focused runtime tests passed the 49/50 boundary and skipped-level finale
cases.
The offscreen, muted `l3-story` app test passed the finale decision cases,
movie handovers, rendered under-50 state and accessibility action target. The
normal L3 chain verifier still passed 15 carried results with three open
chains; its strict mode failed as required at Classic 07, Shadow 106 and
Egyptian 205. This validates the source-level result flow, not a played
three-tribe campaign, exact DOS carry arithmetic, movie soundtrack or
packaged player journey. L3 remains Preview.

### L3 survivor budget from the supplied level headers

Each tribe starts with 20 lemmings. The 30 exact `LEVEL*.DAT` headers add 39
Classic, 36 Shadow and 49 Egyptian lemmings in total. A 50-survivor finish
therefore allows at most 9 losses in Classic, 6 in Shadow and 19 in Egyptian
across the full tribe. These are upper bounds even if every remaining level
has a perfect route. A winning fixture or an extended campaign prefix does
not close the ending gate when its cumulative losses exceed this budget.

`0bc0093be295ae4ff02fd49fb9923f0ba92b5bc6d8d79a8a62bb26ab8dfb3198`.
The repository scripts could not write their `.build` products in this managed
worktree, so the same Swift test and gate sources were compiled into
`/private/tmp` with the macOS 15.4 SDK. No test window or audio was used.

The first linked gaps are now Classic 22/population 20, Shadow 121/population
one and Egyptian 218/population three. The Classic 16 repair retains its prior
ten saved and two lost. The interim Classic 20 repair saves eight and loses
eight at carried population 34, instead of twelve saved and no loss. Classic
21 then saves two and loses eight at population 28. That route can finish with
at most 35 survivors, so a higher-retention Classic 20 and 21 route is still
required. The Shadow 110 standalone win saves two and loses 19; it preserves
win coverage but gives no 50-survivor campaign proof. Egyptian 213/population
22 now saves one, loses ten and retains 12 in reserve. Its level 214/population
13 continuation saves one and loses 15, so Egyptian retention remains open.
Twenty-four standalone L3 wins, three complete 30-level campaigns with at
least 50 final survivors, remaining original-engine parity, media, packaged
saved-run recovery and release-candidate hardware checks remain open. L3 stays
Preview.

A 66-input route now saves all ten released lemmings in Classic 21 and loses

and ends at `4fec23c69dffd60090061f18cc535e293a9e5b024334390555c2db5e5ba6cc66`.

none, keeps 18 in reserve and ends at tick 5,775. Its initial state hash is

and `7f7aed5905737b5523cea541344bd7af3239fa5f78e6ff40bf3b262e0c8e1541`.

`3a01b64e366b290151361e553b8abafe9ea27d96dab17365978de4d8c9bf44ac`.

`f1078ada48528d5c2851b729b241b66e72f70593d885c46c395d33b38ab501ff`.
The first gaps are Classic 22/population 28, Shadow 121/population one and

The linked Classic path still enters Classic 20 at population 34. Its current

shows a retained route if Classic 20 is repaired, but it is not part of the

### 6 October lossless Shadow 108 carried route

At the linked population of 22, a 14-input Shadow 108 route saves all ten
released lemmings and loses none. Twelve stay in reserve, so Shadow 109 starts
with 22. The first scout drops the Umbrella, jumps the first gap, collects the
Bricks box and builds up-right from x303 to reach the upper wall. The second
actor Blocks while the scout establishes the route. After the scout saves, he
resumes walking and each of the nine remaining actors jumps the gap at x213
before following the Brick ramp. The route completes naturally at tick 1,411.

Every input was accepted and the fixture passed two strict replays against
the source-matched global hatch-order engine. Its SHA-256 is
`dfd7cb1d93aa1088ff58698c1754df57caa367e79942c78226402c83187d635f`;
the original level DAT SHA-256 is

fixtures and 58 linked results. The linked record SHA-256 is
`3ae5e0f21cca58f2d55283804e52610eec63f70475cf7deed0a21686296ddd82`.
The standalone manifest remains unchanged.

The linked Shadow 109 standalone fallback accepts the population-22 start
but saves one and loses eleven; the next Shadow 110 fallback saves two and
loses twelve at population 13. The chain therefore still reaches its first
gap at Shadow 121 with one lemming. Higher-retention routes for 109 and 110
are required before this Shadow 108 gain can support a 50-survivor campaign.
The Classic and Egyptian first gaps remain level 22/population 28 and level
218/population three. L3 remains Preview.

### 6 October bundled app check after the DOS hatch-order correction

`Scripts/build-local-app.sh` built a full universal development app from
source commit `270a09646e4fbc7f5b2a6b70ae1e48c9397f9b4e` with Xcode 26.5.
The bundle reports version 1.7.9, build 63. Both `LemmingsLocal` and
`libNxlvKit.dylib` contain arm64 and x86_64 slices. The build uses an ad-hoc
signature. `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passed. The executable SHA-256
is `27e964560d8905dfd246d067e7d7e89cbc223d2b467bab4b627c2d4577365a42`.
The bundled library SHA-256 is
`bf746a71de9af711944d37d0b31c323d624b41919a9f68bacc31689cf79b961c`.

The bundle contains 561 of 576 source L3CD files. The other 15 files are
DOS executables, a batch file and machine settings excluded by
`Scripts/bundle-game-data.sh`. `diff -qr` found no byte difference in the 561
common files. The bundled `LEVEL001.DAT`, `INTRO.FLI` and `THE-END.FLI`
SHA-256 values are `7f4e6fd9b04f6fbcfa4fe3fa6a563458d928ea3ce08f1664a2d633767b5e8c96`,
`eb580c2e63289084ba6253111898601e573d3783195a4169a4314857a0435446`
and `16e796b5e20d76c9f29d65937e081e58ae2c07ddb005a2c9d0a53d05663dcdbd`.

The arm64 and Rosetta x86_64 `Scripts/run-cross-build-recovery-tests.sh`
checks passed with the bundled library and Ports. They checked Classic,
NeoLemmix and L3 legacy replay, continuation and changed-content refusal.
The arm64 and Rosetta x86_64 `Scripts/verify-l3-completion.sh` checks passed
against the bundled library at commit `ba45be4ace83aacfdbbb340b5c67dc299715af4a`.
Both checked 298 fixture hashes, 66 of 90 standalone wins, 141 double-replayed
carried fixtures, 61 linked results and negative gates. The Intel run used a
temporary `swiftc` wrapper with target `x86_64-apple-macosx12.3`. The script
has no target option, and this Xcode driver defaults to arm64 under Rosetta.
Later fixture or runtime commits need a new check against the bundle.

The `TEST_SCOPE=l3-story` test passed with muted audio through the default
offscreen UI runner. Its media path now uses `BundledGameResources.lemmings3()`
from the built app. The test checked opening and ending policy, controls,
input targets, handovers and missing-media behaviour. The captured opening
frame rendered its story card and Pause and Skip controls. The audio probe
checked calls and handover. It did not check audible original-movie fidelity.

This app is a temporary 1.7.9 development build, not a 1.8 release candidate.
Developer ID signing, notarisation, downloaded-archive checks, physical
hardware, complete packaged campaigns and packaged UI saved-run recovery
remain open. L3 stays Preview.

### 6 October original DOS Egyptian 17 stair-path divergence

A headless, muted original DOS CD run loaded Egyptian `217` in the opening
Egyptian slot. The copied `LEVEL217.DAT`, `PERM217.OBS` and `TEMP217.OBS`
matched the supplied source SHA-256 values
`9ee72008901c237e7d067e1bde2090b67252e3d1b3b63b6a893447499268fc0b`,
`0d34ee97097609faaf79ddeebc3a5e2640a5390331b063f4a2ad5a2c252ecb21`
and `a4933725b172286e51171589069f0b4db3edec1d6f64451d88602cd14f2d877a`.
The disposable game copy, SDL injector, capture and decoder are in
`/private/tmp/l3-egypt217-dos-20261006`. Dummy SDL video and audio, software
rendering and `nosound=true` kept the run offscreen and silent. DOSBox exited
cleanly. The gameplay AVI SHA-256 is
`ccbd9a1e9b9ab898af4bd824cf6ebb27f852484968709328673ea4fc4b311291`;
the decoded frame 170, 310 and 400 SHA-256 values are
`b5d88c4c57606f01e1845c0470cf43afa5866d4880e5aac5c9513f0784d4d843`,
`b1fea6818c2272c88985cc28e35987862bf29c94bc0558df603c181161b93639`
and `20cd9b590e3adfb772838b34799cf8483ee3f26d5aad96c56e4bd867e98fea26`.

The first left-hatch actor's red sprite occupies x 56–63 above the
lowest fragile stair in frame 170. It ascends the next stairs and reaches
the blue structure by frame 400 without a tool input. The source-built native
runtime instead starts this actor at x 52 and holds that x while falling past
the first stair; it reaches y 92 by tick 72. The source stair tops are y 66
at x 56–63, y 64 at x 64–71 and y 60 at x 72–79, all tagged `0x2020`.
The original frames and native ticks are not time-aligned. The capture proves
the visible passive path differs, but does not by itself identify the full
collision rule or a winning route.

A disposable global hatch-x `+8` prototype, source SHA-256
`4fd55a8167bb2f5417b7a28ad98639577c8da89ac943d0d9931e2bc0a806ec8c`
and dylib SHA-256
`1dd6bcc45a525be4880d8f6a7fde8f601a4cf2ffa14a08b23be351d4f0a1f95a`,
lands the native actor on the first stair. It changes the outcome or accepted
input count for 173 of the then-recorded 207 standalone and carried
fixtures, so it was not promoted. A second private prototype also checks thin
source-brick tops above the next foot contact. Its source and dylib SHA-256
values are
`9f7f65c18259967ca3b0e1702effb4e4d2eac197ee9c7ff986844ecffb6211bb`
and `1243ff4063da72d0d4f7bec5ca1fe38fbf699cfc97c09abc5f8a7eec2e740f4c`.
The actor then reaches x 75/y 60 at native tick 80 and x 95/y 52 at tick 100,
but the existing carried population-seven route saves only two of eleven
active actors. This prototype remains private. Original sprite anchoring,
stair contact, affected-route migration and 50-survivor progression need
further exact evidence. L3 remains Preview.

A separate physical hatch-x `+4` prototype has source and dylib SHA-256 values
`d70a67b1f35470dde93fc9689952b02872691a4471d2b79c14a0913b4dcd3d83`
and `10923448ad22531a6ef8bba8739c475155162e124de80167a464b5b9144a2a5f`.
It also lands the first Egyptian 17 actor on the x 56/y 66 stair. That actor
walks to x 71/y 64, then falls at x 72 because the next thin stair starts at
y 60. The `+4` prototype changes accepted inputs or outcomes for 157 of the
same 207 fixtures: 60 Classic, 37 Shadow and 60 Egyptian. A private `+4`
prototype that also detects the thin source-brick top has source and dylib
SHA-256 values
`a97b8966c8aaeb49363dbcc6b4faa65f67de6f72a95aacd47c1b72d8cd22007f`
and `329b5622aade389c631d1d9b6c0df4c08629217ab7bdd383fc27810a6898ed36`.
It reaches x 103/y 50 at native tick 112 but turns at the next blue metal
rise. In the original DOS run, the actor's red sprite advances from x 97–103
in frame 350 to x 113–119 in frame 400 without a tool input; frame 350
SHA-256 is `e374dad4bc647e98e6920b1a2f3cade10677ddd68a1833c2e56d6b0b0f266bb9`.
This is a second observed contact mismatch on the same passive path. The
private `+4` plus thin-step prototype saves only two of eleven active actors
with the existing population-seven route. It was not promoted. At this stage,
the exact DOS collision point, sprite offset and impact on retained routes
were unresolved.

Source-bank sprite matching supersedes the `+4` hatch-anchor hypothesis above.
The unchanged `TRIBE005.IND` and `TRIBE005.CMP` SHA-256 values are
`ae602a07feeb0abec146ed15379f09bb59e68c3fae4690cdb368c681c477306d`
and `58f5eeb413fc1307b751f3055106be43d4effc1d5ad0551a32988ef86704e49f`.
Against the same exact DOS AVI, the 95 actor pixels changed in the left-hatch
region between decoded frames 140 and 160 uniquely match animation 9,
frame 6 opaque mask at draw origin x 52/y 32, with no missing or extra pixels;
the next-best mask differs at 14 pixels. The native renderer draws a 16-by-16
actor at physical `x - 8, y - 16`, so physical actor position x 60/y 48 matches
that original draw origin. This supports the private `+8` hatch contact model
over `+4` for this opening path. The decoded frame 160 SHA-256 is
`7e348e5ecc5e54dadbc3a07c584f5d9d76fa09ae2f425780b5cf5d38cc26ed32`.

At the blue rise, frames 350, 375 and 400 match source-bank sprite masks at
draw origins x 94/y 34, x 101/y 26 and x 110/y 22 with no mask error. The
only other 22 changed
pixels in each sampled region are the worn fragile stair at x 85–95/y 52–57.
Using the same sprite anchor gives physical positions x 102/y 50, x 109/y 42
and x 118/y 38. The private `+8` hatch, thin source-brick contact and
three-cell blue-rise prototype reaches those positions at native ticks 107,
114 and 123. Original video frames and native ticks have not been aligned.
At x 103/y 50, the next x 104/y 42 metal step is eight pixels high and three
of the four projected class cells are solid. The current four-cell threshold
reverses; a three-cell threshold crosses this observed step. The private
prototype source and library SHA-256 values are
`63d99ccdc795ec9831b2ad6736d5c50748daf2a9a0de9cec50c9fce79cd3b0dd`
and `d5cc7928ef29543dda6fa695536f1b48e3c13d48f9a90ab90954d45345a97edc`.
The private population-seven route derived from the carried population-one
fixture accepts both inputs under this prototype but saves none and loses 11
released actors. It is not a winning carried route.

Against the current 209 standalone and carried fixtures, the committed
runtime retains all 209 numeric outcomes and input sequences. The combined
private prototype rejects an input in 145, consumes all inputs but changes
the final tick or survivor numbers in 47, and retains those numbers in 17.
All fixture identities would need regeneration after a hatch-coordinate
change. The source-bank extraction, matching script and impact logs are in
`/private/tmp/l3-egypt217-dos-20261006`; the two impact-log SHA-256 values
are `353db68d61b5e2d9d989d5a9d15ff3f3f283b458d3c6d58c24ccf8a0f8f9ff56`
and `7510b9ede98e5fd2e1bd8b16833f0620943cdc99ad4d3d849f31683b0df6eb43`.
The prototype remains private while all affected routes, original timing and
continuous survivor retention are checked. L3 remains Preview.

An isolated physical hatch `+8` build, without either terrain-rule change,
has source SHA-256
`cf08dec207f55a7f414d2b1ec9cb35655a3fee136319ef9bf709f57d740b68dd`
and library SHA-256
`aa46826cdcd43720f9efd08a7ee9d2dcf85ebbc603c7610d14aebf4683b3730e`.
Across the same 209 fixtures it rejects 145 input sequences, changes the
terminal tick or survivor numbers for 41 consumed sequences, and retains those
numbers for 23. Adding the thin source-brick rule changes 15 detailed audit

changes only the two Egyptian 17 records, which were already changed by the

SHA-256 values
`b58bb976c0ebe9284a3c258839d619e9319606554c7c03e4bd436eaea8050270`
and `746113bfa1ba8c1f45f42e871f59f05c55928a23a00c865a0ccb93fa022e7a2c`.
The broad migration cost comes mainly from the hatch-coordinate correction.

gaps at level 121/population one and Egyptian at level 221/population two.
Twenty-three standalone wins, the complete 30-level campaigns with at least
50 survivors, original DOS parity and the remaining package and hardware
gates remain open. L3 stays Preview.

| Zero-based DOS frame | Source animation/frame | Sprite draw origin | Matched actor pixels |
| --- | --- | --- | ---: |
| 160 | 9/6, falling | x260/y48 | 88/88 |
| 285 | 0/10, walking | x283/y60 | 75/75 |

### 6 October solver scoring for multiple hatches

Commit `d7e9574` scores unreleased actors from the hatch that the current
native runtime will use for each release. It accounts for completed hatch
cycles and the offset after actors have already emerged. Previously, the
solver scored every reserve actor from the first hatch, which misranked
multi-hatch candidates. Commit `9fc7049` stops stale fixture checks when a
level completes before their checkpoint. The solver and current focused test
source SHA-256 values are
`1f9f2205d89c1c0fc6d01e472ac04de31a51502c275a9220469f5abccd3cc077`
and `1fed28a7fcc0a4044b04d504f051fce9091b5b1a0dccddfd885b7c49efe48f6a`.

The headless, muted `Scripts/run-lemmings3-solver-tests.sh` passes all nine
focused checks. The test now uses the current lossless Classic 03 and 06
fixtures and the retained Egyptian 18 same-tick two-actor input. Its old
Shadow 02 checkpoint waited past the current route's completion tick and
could not terminate. A synthetic two-hatch case checks the score before and
after the first release. This fixes ranking and the stale test only; it
does not establish a new winning route or original DOS hatch cadence. The
full L3 completion gate remains 67/90 standalone wins, 142 carried fixtures
and 62 linked results. L3 stays Preview.

### 6 October scheduled-release solver score and source-matched gate

Commit `7e47d7c` exposes the runtime's current pending hatch-release count
and uses it in the solver distance score. The runtime initially schedules
up to ten hatch actors. It schedules another actor only when a released
actor dies, up to the level's total population. The earlier score treated
all reserve actors as scheduled, including survivors who might never emerge.
The focused headless, muted solver script passes ten checks, including a
two-hatch population-20 case that distinguishes ten scheduled releases from
twenty reserve actors. The runtime, solver and test source SHA-256 values are
`e51bd9596bd2a1c10c0395691260525bfcda73876fa2aa3299975948ff2c5023`,
`46c1a0bcbd023f9d10e4e62a0e0cbb2fe6748919dde7f27962160837952af7b8`
and `14cf5bf8705d24808774373d9a51b6f6fe02c34ffde1d026ab065cab9ae04efe`.

With `L3_TEST_LIBRARY_DIR` set to the source-matched
`.build/l3-solver-tests` library, `Scripts/verify-l3-completion.sh` passes
299 manifest hashes, 67 of 90 standalone wins, 142 double-replayed carried
fixtures, 62 linked results and the negative gates. The arm64 test library
SHA-256 is `bd5dea5bf26312064a04fbbb4dcf0c737f39972ee7a5750b0e4d78b1c3fe29d9`.
The manifest and linked record SHA-256 values remain
`4ef0c7d8cb9de2f04a1adb43d2aaaa1208a8dd326c024666193eefb4d795218f`
and `732da5a0c374a3af577f833a5e71c57c23c49043f16b338ae7fd55ffc826b524`.
An initial run used the older default `.build/local/arm64` library
(`7a92a9d19adc60a57d3f47e1d420e8c836ab91468d4affd5883152f3aa82f1b8`)
and failed at a changed Classic 05 replay input. The source-matched rerun
passed; the first result is a local build mismatch, not a runtime regression.

A bounded headless, muted search with the corrected score found no new
winning candidate for Egyptian 21 or 24 in 30 seconds each. It expanded
258,822 and 504,363 candidates respectively. These time-limited results
do not show that either level is impossible. The 23 missing standalone
wins, all three continuous 50-survivor campaigns, original DOS parity and
the remaining release-candidate gates remain open. L3 stays Preview.

### 6 October Shadow 10 independent edge-fall trace

The unmodified DOS capture and `TRIBE010.IND`, `.CMP` and `.PAL` sources have
the SHA-256 values recorded in the Shadow 10 trace above. The capture SHA-256
is `e5ed498f007af5f313947074cf556db4f40a1112a062c8bc4fa73cae8aa978f9`.
The AVI header records 14,268 microseconds per frame, or about 70.086 frames
per second. Each checked actor image persists for three AVI frames. Matching
the decoded images against the original sprite masks gives zero actor-pixel
error for these draw origins:

| Platform edge | DOS frames, one image per three frames | Sprite draw x | Sprite draw y |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Upper, moving right | 301, 304, 307, 310, 313, 316 | 289, 289, 290, 292, 292, 292 | 60, 62, 64, 66, 68, 72 |
| Lower, moving left | 457, 460, 463, 466, 469, 472 | 279, 279, 278, 276, 276, 276 | 124, 126, 128, 130, 132, 136 |

At both edges, the original sprite keeps the walking image for one state
at the edge, then falls at the same draw x. The next four images move down
two pixels each. The fifth moves down four. The fall draw x moves by one
and then two pixels in the travel direction. The current native state
change moves zero pixels down. Later fall updates use
`min(4, 1 + age / 3)`, which starts with three one-pixel steps.

A private prototype applies an eight-pixel hatch correction, defers the
unsupported-edge state change by one update, and uses the measured edge-fall
draw steps. With a constant `x − 8, y − 16` draw anchor, its coordinates match
both DOS sequences. Its runtime source and library SHA-256 values are
`9fe08dc88505d550aede2e03ec369d0cf2f20997e8231a6b641516990ac5f687`
and `2f61a66a15cb0732770ec9ec295855d75491a3dd4d6a2c380be52627b61f8690`.
The original executable's stored actor coordinates and sprite-anchor rules
remain unverified. The 64-byte `TRIBE010.AUX` file has no native parser and
no verified DOS offset meaning. The visual match does not yet prove collision
or route parity.

A headless, muted impact audit compared accepted inputs and numeric results
for a 209-fixture snapshot with 67 standalone and 142 carried routes. The
source-matched engine retained all 209 records. The private prototype
rejects an input in 163, changes the result or tick in 36, and retains
those numbers in ten. Among the 54 Shadow fixtures, 36 reject an input,
14 change the result or tick, and four retain those numbers.
Fifty Shadow records differ from the current engine. Both Shadow 10 fixtures
reject their second input at tick 110. The impact output SHA-256 is
`ac147508707087fcd6184edb52335cff47b5af1ce6a070de5ab38679adf232c5`.
Retained numbers do not establish strict replay because the hatch position
changes the initial state hash. The prototype remains private until original
coordinate and contact evidence and repaired double-replayed routes support
a shared rule. L3 stays Preview.

ran with dummy video and audio, software output, `nosound=true` and no

Exact RGB comparison of original frames 900, 1100, 1300 and 1500 against

cell. Each has two eight-pixel approach steps and a 40-pixel landing that
ends in source air. A similar Classic 11 contact has an original Brick at
that landing's far cell. The final terrain-shape rule uses this source
cell to keep a worn Brick gap from changing the stair behaviour. A broad
one-cell prototype failed 17 of 209 retained fixtures, and an earlier
shape rule failed three Classic 11 fixtures. The final rule double-replays

### 6 October Egyptian 13 Mole patrol divergence

SHA-256 values are
`68932677dea3deace2a41af328d3003d6c4baf19db411cdf8177eed048d0f78d`,

and `1f313694b43d3ad028e2951f9e16d9b0ab7dd68631bad6b9963fe86f29bc5aeb`.
The permanent object file places identifier `10013` at `(136,76)`, which the
native parser maps to a Mole at `(144,94)` facing right. A disposable DOS CD
copy loaded the level in the opening slot. Its permanent and temporary object
files are byte identical to the supplied 213 files; the 30-byte level header
differs only at offsets 6 and 8, where those object references change from

`e45b46ee13c18d0ae55183a71eaa69d1a2061b8495056cfc7dadcff1388cd979`.

x90–102 at frame 100, x46–58 at frame 200 and x31–44 near the left turn at
frame 230. They return to x125–137 near frame 480 and x31–44 near frame
695. These are pixel bounds, not decoded stored actor coordinates. Exact RGB
comparison of frame 100 against frames 500, 1,000, 2,000, 3,000 and 9,000
finds zero changed pixels in the x20–140, y80–120 dirt region below the
patrol. This run shows repeated left/right travel without excavation there.
The current native Mole initially travels right and digs across the pyramid,
so its Egyptian 13 route evidence does not reproduce this original behaviour.

fixtures. Among 209 records common to the earlier +8 hatch and stair audit,
only four Egyptian 213 records change. The old carried
population-22 route accepts all ten inputs but saves zero rather than one.
The audit log SHA-256 is
`217f047679718c70a88b4e1cd1ed8fb82370860148e13591c3fbb737ec8f482d`.
This patrol prototype and the broader +8 hatch/stair candidate remain private.
An original contact-triggered Mole burrow capture, a new Egyptian 13 winning

The retained Egyptian 206 route now saves zero because it relied on automatic

`3286b83af83596afa17e27cfbff18eb02ee830d8c91e90b7d505ae70237b9443`.

upper route. It then takes a Spade, cuts through the middle ledge and takes
the Spade pickup at x128/y180. It cuts a shallow left tunnel so the lower
mob descends safely, then cuts a shaft to the exit. A lower blocker turns
that mob towards the exit. The upper pair are released after the Potato
Beast patrol has moved clear. All ten actors exit without a loss. This is a
native strict route; original DOS input, collision and trap parity for this
level has not yet been measured.

The linked Classic chain now enters level 25 with population 36. The
regenerated manifest SHA-256 is
`1f81efb19d7518b70a32ea35a0d12b58643c2b79a1556ff544ade6e7fca4d92c`
and the linked chain record SHA-256 is
`4d69cd817af7fa6ca6379f48c43a0913f43d3321d348b725ccbd6348c0cecf8f`.
The source-matched, headless and muted full gate passes 301 fixture hashes,
69 of 90 standalone wins, malformed-route rejection, 144 double-replayed
carried fixtures and 64 linked results. Its log SHA-256 is
`20ed39edc5302c2d48f10b1456221dbc8983e4f308268b40b47214c3bb8a090a`.
The first gaps are Classic 25/population 36, Shadow 121/population one and
Egyptian 221/population two. Twenty-one standalone wins and all three
50-survivor campaigns remain open. L3 stays Preview.

### 6 October bundled L3 saved-run app recovery

The focused `TEST_SCOPE=l3-recovery` app integration test passed through
`Scripts/run-app-integration-tests.sh` and the default offscreen UI runner
with `LEMMINGS_TEST_AUDIO=muted`. It built the current app source on arm64
against the existing local 1.7.9 development bundle resources. The test app
executable SHA-256 is
`581d0f3d6fdf3f662f3f1dc02ead36dc327f4eb13b93c439694adf2ba5e20f14`;
the bundled `Trolley/engine-fingerprint.txt` SHA-256 is

and `2d07bd80604ed37ae49dc4fcaa40ef089b7e16a4e28992`.

On bundled Classic 1, the app accepted a Blocker input at tick 120, saved an
in-progress disk checkpoint at tick 150, then restored the exact paused
simulation, selected action, input journal and run ID. Replaying 40 further

package, recovery across an installed update, or physical interruption.
The full 90-level route, survivor, parity, media and hardware gates remain
open. L3 stays Preview.

### 6 October replay action timing audit

`Scripts/audit-l3-action-ticks.py` scans the current strict L3 fixture files
and groups accepted actions by tick and lemming. It found repeated actions on
one lemming in one tick in 17 of 70 standalone fixtures. Those files contain
1,669 repeated lemming-tick groups and 1,671 actions after each group's first
action. It found the same pattern in 29 of 145 carried fixtures, with 59
groups and 63 further actions. The largest group has three actions.

Egyptian `228.json` has 1,634 of the standalone groups. Its 4,540 Walker and
Jumper actions span 4,140 ticks. It saves one, loses 23 and has no reserve.
The fixture SHA-256 is
`e497bff2f28e8946855cfc5c8eb1fb80206c7475e4b93052c6eb92a8dc5509e8`.
The audit script SHA-256 is
`f2ee6238a9658516250e4c1428ae08afafe8e857c144585ffe650f1ad0ae3e1e`.
Its ignored report is `.build/l3-action-ticks-20261006.json`, SHA-256
`648d3b5df8a950c1ea70e251c6a5d137854cfb4c02e91e67d2661e760fefb55b`.
The audit reads fixture JSON only. It does not change game data or replays.

The strict native replay accepts these ordered actions. No matched original
DOS input trace yet shows whether a player can assign each sequence within
one logic tick. This audit does not establish that a level or route is
impossible. Original input timing and route parity remain open, especially
for the high-frequency Egyptian 28 fallback. The new audit adds no win and
does not change the 70-of-90 count or Preview label.

### 8 October Classic 26 documented vertical-stack route check

The complete Classic 26 route in the [Lemmings Chronicles walkthrough](https://www.cheatbook.de/wfiles/lemmingschronicles.htm) specifies three up-right Bricks, followed by a four-Brick vertical stack that should place the scout on the upper ledge. A headless, muted native-runtime replay tested that exact opening at carried population 40. It retained the previously verified five-input opening, used up-right on lemming 2 at tick 457, then vertical at tick 475 and Walker at tick 525 to stop immediately after the fourth vertical Brick.

The exact input record is `/private/tmp/l3-classic26-guide-fourvertical40.json`, SHA-256 `43f9a15ac80aa9c22ea9fb8193d84b3e1aa9effb66c239ec9ac44907f80ffe6e`. It uses supplied `LEVEL026.DAT`, SHA-256 `17ab2b1c30ba7b11d7b307c398b3e443956a5aa65b45dd9abfebdc589080da22`, from the same initial-state SHA-256 `24a8bf62b8f5a4ab1e6293d5a7d65037d8f8a89c83c637fefe4a62de30c3acd5` as the carried prefix. The source-matched solver and `NxlvKit` library SHA-256 values are `20bc73ac280a1944e7c8ed594ec6b17bd762461f2985207eae9e844bffab93a1` and `cb9e5f9eccf88d7898129e0adda5b6793729fa4f6bb6822b8da9e03645c202b0`.

At tick 524 the lemming is at `(298,148)` with one Brick remaining. The Walker action at tick 525 is accepted. It reaches `(304,148)`, falls at tick 531 and drowns at tick 547. The trace SHA-256 is `979418330ec1308ba9ce6dc4ea93b2059a4ddea5dd7b56121bb4e9969f9d1473`. Allowing the fifth vertical Brick also falls at the same x-coordinate, so the route does not produce a native candidate. This establishes neither an original-DOS discrepancy nor an impossibility result: the walkthrough is a route lead, and exact original-DOS input, movement and terrain evidence is still required before changing native Brick behaviour. Classic 26, its population-40 campaign link and the release gate remain open; Lemmings 3 remains Preview.

### 8 October Classic 30 walkthrough opening

The source walkthrough's opening can now be reproduced in the current native runtime. At tick 69, Walker turns hatch lemming 2 left; it collects the umbrella at tick 99, floats to the lower ledge and collects the first Bricks at tick 259. Jumper at tick 273 crosses the first gap. The lemming collects the adjacent Brick boxes at tick 306, for 32 Bricks, reverses at tick 313, and a vertical Brick use at tick 321 places two Bricks before the current native geometry stops the build at tick 354. Using that checked seed through tick 321, a headless and muted fine-placement continuation used a four-pixel tool-site cell, paired-actor branches, beam 64 and fewer-inputs-first ordering. selection brackets then cleared. After one second, it detected the leading

cleared. The source had executed both inputs before the first later still.

second actor. It used `(328,486)` then `(265,658)` for the leading actor. The

### 8 October L3 original carrier-selection controls

The original game manual records a distinct carried-tool control path: left
click selects a Lemming, right click highlights a tool carrier in red, and a
right click away from a carrier cycles through active carriers. The native L3
canvas now implements that path. A right click on a carrier selects it; a
right click away from a carrier cycles active carriers in stable actor order.
The selected carrier has a persistent red marker. When that carrier overlaps
the left-click target group and can accept the selected action, it takes
priority over the normal targeting preferences.

This change is limited to selection. It does not change pickup, Drop,
movement, tool quantity or trap rules. The original control evidence is the
All New World of Lemmings manual, page 59,
`https://www.lemonamiga.com/doc/all-new-world-of-lemmings/59`.
The manual does not establish the original carrier-cycle ordering.

The headless, muted `Scripts/run-lemmings3-targeting-tests.sh` check passed
the manual-carrier priority, direct-carrier selection and mid-air carrier
cycle assertions. The headless, muted compile-only sequel check also compiled
the changed AppKit source. The relevant source SHA-256 values are
`0e4ab57ace28381552387de5c58ff5d364d7bab2977c9d18204444aefcb658c2`
for `Lemmings3PlayWindow.swift`,
`1bf0db8ace61496e0abe1a2e822f22a60411704bd58da4cac9fd743cda5c0df4`
for `Lemmings3Targeting.swift`, and
`7429d5ee15823d0ad147213f2361d96465245b12c0e587995acd289c949b85c6`
for its focused test.

This closes no original-DOS behaviour gate. Exact original UI artwork,
physical-input confirmation, the remaining route fixtures, all three
50-survivor campaigns, release-candidate recovery and packaging gates remain
open. L3 remains Preview.

### 8 October Shadow 26 Brick-drop and upper-right Spade continuation

The current runtime accepts the guide-derived Brick drop at tick 315. The same
worker then obtains eight Spades at tick 360. A direction sweep at that
checkpoint shows that the up-right Spade branch removes 256 native terrain
cells and sends the worker into a fall; up-left and up remove 128 cells, right
removes 256, down-left and down remove 192, and left removes none. The
upper-right branch follows an early interpretation of the guide's described
cut. A later tail trace shows that it drops the worker to death at tick 389, so
it is not a viable native route prefix. This identifies a rejected native
search branch only. It does not establish the original input direction,
terrain mask, timing or outcome.

### 8 October Shadow 26 native Brick-prefix refinement

The vertical-Spade branch gives a concrete native Brick experiment. After the
worker drops the remaining Spades at tick 370, the worker reclaims eight
Bricks at tick 413. Reversing the worker at that tick and selecting vertical
Bricks at tick 456 raises the worker from `y=242` to `y=226` and consumes four
Bricks. At tick 521 the worker cannot make the next vertical placement because
of remaining source terrain, falls to `y=234`, then turns between `x=200` and
`x=201`. It therefore does not reach the exit.

The wall-direction sweep establishes that vertical Bricks are the only tested
Brick direction that edits terrain at the `x=198,y=242` contact point. The
bridge trace and wall-direction sweep source/transcript SHA-256 values are
`736185c9c9add4c54c422d6024ff5b8588b8e4632589187826a48b99d4563c8e`,
`4ead8718aff6ccc9a52d94628ed52dc2f89e0d23a3f15e7718c7fc122929d09c`,
`d8f5964363d6e8b08bf206cb780ab528f7f1027e4307fe941c1d9e68e900721a`
and `203b90f0e04cf80994dfd70479d4c0df18ff0284cc8edb21b4ba2667b3628020`.
The retained files are
`/private/tmp/l3-shadow126-bridge-up-probe-20261008.{swift,txt}` and
`/private/tmp/l3-shadow126-bridge-wall-sweep-20261008.{swift,txt}`.

This is a native prefix diagnostic. Original-DOS evidence is required before
changing the Brick rules or treating the stall as a parity defect. It does not
provide a route. Shadow 126, its campaign link and the L3 Preview gate remain
open.

### 8 October Shadow 26 original-DOS carrier and Use-order probe

A fresh headless, muted original-DOS run used the same byte-identical
relocated Shadow 26 data and the established six menu clicks. A right click at
`(456,466)` changed the fourth panel cell to an orange building-talent icon
with the displayed count `8`. This agrees with the supplied manual's
description of the eight-use building talent. A later right click at
`(550,475)` did not retain that panel state. This identifies `(456,466)` as a
source tool carrier at this observed scene time. It does not identify the
carrier's level role or align the source and native clocks.

The manual says to select panel cell four and then click the talent holder.
The first unpaused source run therefore performed: right click the carrier,
click `(371,650)`, left click the carrier, click `(456,420)`, then drag from
`(456,466)` to `(456,420)`. The paused variant used the same order, resumed
after the upward click, and ran for three seconds. Neither run consumed the
displayed eight uses, changed terrain, or produced a tool pose. The click and
drag only moved the source target marker. These results reject the earlier
claim that the target-first sequence tested a known carrier. They do not show
which source control chooses a building direction.

The carrier-identification injector and staged image are

### 8 October Egyptian 24 Buzzard route diagnostic

The retained guide says that the first lemming crosses the upper route, collects
Bricks, waits for the Buzzard to become safe, continues right, drops Bricks,
then returns left to collect them. This is a walkthrough lead, not original DOS
engine evidence: [Lemmings Chronicles walkthrough](https://www.cheatbook.de/wfiles/lemmingschronicles.htm).

The checked native prefix reaches the same upper pickup. At tick 460, the
first lemming has eight Bricks at `(452,80)`. A diagnostic built from the
same decoded level with only the level-224 Buzzard removed allows that actor
to collect Bricks, drop them at tick 500, turn at the right edge, and recover
the dropped box at tick 620. It therefore confirms that the guide's next
pickup sequence is feasible in the current terrain and tool model when the
current Buzzard interaction is absent. It does not prove that the original
Buzzard is inert or establish its contact rule.

A separate population-one blocker sweep tested the native interpretation of
the guide's wait. It assigned a Blocker at every second tick from 416 through
438, then a Walker at every tenth tick from 470 through 760. No accepted
freeze and release pair survived to tick 900. The selected run at tick 420
shows the current Buzzard reaching the blocking lemming before the release.
This is a current-model result. It does not establish the original control,
movement or collision behaviour.

The level SHA-256 is
`4c6459be8889e1ed67f05b21614e9c47a4fd8289ccf8244ab08af5c624595cc1`.
The inert-creature diagnostic source, executable and transcript SHA-256
values are
`4f161fd65b56337e42987ce4200a79a3df9c0db0fa80b16d6864dd8145820496`,
`4487067d14714f01d39473c59077bd2151fa8c43533f23510eed96e6f2653825`
and
`f9d0fcdfac8a9c9792647ae71ee1b5a2bfedd7413aa6e1254403e54e5aa33728`.
The blocker sweep source, executable and empty transcript SHA-256 values are
`87bf303fabdbc0ac2ec373b82fa5239cde3e07e629240afe7468f81c74822ff2`,
`07f43097724dfccb7ff3f41679f4b95ee2d9ac676b7ae21f2eebddc9686e8f57`
and
`e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855`.
They linked against the current runtime source and library SHA-256 values
`24208dcd68a23b31fdbc4ac70fa166174c64393348dfeefdc314c78e894b26b1`
and
`6093fe2f114c7c67233e1778196c582b3802296e05078c71e824eea6ff054075`.

No runtime, fixture, campaign result or manifest entry changed. An original
headless action-and-contact capture is still required before a level-specific
Buzzard rule can change. Egyptian 24, its carried campaign route and the L3
Preview gate remain open.
